## 29. The review of I2 (fixer, 2026-10-06): the gate renders the wired page and holds every price to the server's rule

**What was found.** The review of I2 planted fourteen defects, each in a line that only CONNECTS a part to the catalogue (the hero's `pricePending`, the FAQ's count, the group line's guard, the several-eyes card's count, the price table's sale, ordering's sale) or in the VALUE of a price that was in the right place (the black tile and the black row printing the art price, the Trio printing the price of two eyes). The gate rendered `StyleTile`, `PriceTableView` and `HeroView` handed a catalogue by the gate itself, so none of the wrappers and none of `ordering.ts` was ever run; it also asked only that a price was there, not what it said. All fourteen are confirmed: the tree before the fix passes the gate on every one of them (the new defect test, run on an export of the tip before this fix, gave 0 of 14 as expected: the old gate and the production build stay green).

**What was done.** `src/landing/ordering.ts` now makes its store with `createOrdering(ask, settled)`; the page's one instance asks the real server, the hooks read the store through `OrderingContext`. The gate (`src/landing/shell/gate.tsx`) makes a store of its own per state of the catalogue, with a stand-in server, and renders the WIRED components: `StyleGallery` (every group, so every line and every several-eyes card), `PriceTable`, `HeroSection`, `Faq`, `SiteTop` (the bar), `Pricing` (the notice and the payment line) and `HowItWorks` (the third step), through the same hooks the live page uses, so the page's asking code (health, checkout, `ordersOpen`, `saleCatalogue`) runs in the build. `LangProvider` takes an initial language and `StyleGallery` an initial group (nothing else of the live page changes). `scripts/landing_gate.mjs` reads the lines, the cards, the FAQ, the bar, the notice and the steps from the markup, and holds the VALUE of every price the page prints (a tile, a row of the table, the hero's "from", the further-eye price of the card) to the server's own rule: `check_landing_assets.mjs` `expectedPrices` works the price of every style for every count of eyes out of `markets.priceMinor` and the registry's price class, so the landing's own price helpers and the gallery's price kinds are not trusted. A seventh state, "every style live, beyond the ceilings" (`scripts/lib/stubapi.mjs`, ticked `'beyond'`), is rendered too: the several-eyes ladder, the card's price and the pairs' prices are only ever printed in such a state, so they are held to the truth before a ceiling is raised. Vite's config-less module runner cannot transform a stylesheet and the chapter's components import their own, so the Node-side loaders stub stylesheets (`scripts/lib/cssstub.mjs`: `renderLanding`, `run_ts_tests.mjs`).

**The defect test** (`npm run check:gate`): 13 cases became **27**, each in a throw-away copy with a real production build: the old 13, 8 defects in the wiring (hero always shown, hero "from" the ladder's lowest, FAQ counting eyes nobody can buy, FAQ in its open wording while closed, group line without its guard, several-eyes card sure of three eyes, price table widened to three eyes, ordering handing the page the whole catalogue), 3 more in the other places that say whether ordering is open (the bar, the pricing notice, the third step), 3 in the value of a price. **27 of 27 as expected**: every defect fails the gate and the production build, the honest page and the planned-style control pass, the same defect in a preview build is a notice. `--no-build` (the part `v3reg` runs) takes about a minute.

**Counts that moved (none lowered).** `ts` 426 to **442** (+16: the page rendered wired in four states, the group lines read from the chapter's markup, the cards, the FAQ answer and question, the bar, the notice and the steps, the hero's line against the ladder, `createOrdering` run against ten stand-in answers, the hooks' first paint); `v3reg` 212 to **216** (+4: the readers, the value of a price, the wiring of the chapter, the other places that say whether ordering is open; the gate's states are seven; the 27 cases). `suites/baseline.json` follows.

**Bytes.** The store as a function and a context cost **+217 B** of gzip JavaScript (`check:budget`: +7,655 B over the page without motion, was +7,438 B; the hard stop is +8,000, so **345 B** are left, they were 562 B); the CSS is unchanged at +7,179 B. The legal pages /terms /privacy /imprint /withdrawal (36 views) are identical in pixels, every box, every computed style and text to a build of the commit before this fix.

**Checks** (on the tree with the fix, the other builder's motion work in the same worktree): `tsc -b` clean, `oxlint` exit 0 (no new warning in the files of this fix), `npm run build` exit 0 in preview mode and with `VERCEL_ENV=production` (registry hash `76ffb8838041`; the gate prints nothing), `check:prices`, `check:texts`, `check:styles`, `check:assets` (7 states, 4 languages, about 2 s) green; `check:gate` 27 of 27 with a real production build per case; `check:states` against the stub 2416 of 2416 (4 states, 4 languages, 2 widths) and against the real dev API 1208 of 1208 (English and German); `check:landing` exit 0 (phone LCP 1092, 1096, 1120 ms, CLS 0.0004, the one known note of the A$ offer after 3 s), with axe-core 0 violations in 8 states; `check:shell` 0 pixels, `check:slots`, `check:motion`, `check:reveal`, `check:taste` green; the legal pages (36 views) identical to a build of the commit before the fix. **Full set** on the working tree (private fixtures, three suites at a time, scratch on drive D): **36 of 36 green, every count equal to `suites/baseline.json`** (the shared tree also held the other builder's ten new motion tests: `ts` 452 = 442 + 10).

## 30. The review fix of the motion (I3 fixer, 2026-10-06): the slider in short windows, the frame while a picture decodes, the arrival that is spent

**What was found.** The review of `57a275b` (ship, no blocker) named a major: the plain slider of the result never swept, and its handle stayed at 92 for ever (the customer's photo over the restored picture), wherever the window is shorter than the slider, which is a square as wide as the page: a phone held sideways (635 px in 375) and a page zoomed to 200 percent (608 px in 360). The observer was asked for 60 percent of the slider, a share such a window cannot show, and the first report of the observer cancelled the failsafe, so nothing could put the handle at 50 any more. The review's list of majors reached the fixer without its entries (the summary says two); the one it describes is that one, and the minors that stand on the same ground were fixed with it: m1 (the frame of an arriving picture was black and mute for as long as the decode took), m2 (a fade of its own on the style tiles, in no row of table 7.5) and m4 (an arrival left unspent when a style was chosen during the opening, so that the frame would open again when it was mounted again, for instance when an eye is taken away). Left as they were, with the reason: m3 (`/order` opened ready: the words fade, so the text paints 500 ms later; the largest paint, the picture, is unchanged), m5 (the buy card arrives by fade-up, which is the row "Buy card" of 7.5; only the card as a whole arrives, nothing inside it has motion of its own), m6 (the "before" bytes of `check:motion:flow` are written in the script, as its own comment says).

**What was done.** `CompareSlider`: seen is 60 percent of the slider or, where the slider is taller than the window, 60 percent of what the window can show of it (`sweepNeed`, pure, in `flowLogic.ts`); the observer is given that threshold (`[0, need / 2, need]`) and asked again when the window changes size; a slider with half of what it needs in the window counts as seen after 3.5 s (`SWEEP_PARTIAL_MS`, never left half hidden); one that is out of the window, or only peeks in, waits for the customer, so its one sweep is not spent on nobody; the dead observer keeps its failsafe (50 after 3.5 s). `ArtImage`: a `waiting` prop, given by `ResultView` (the composing status and hairline) while the first picture of an arrival decodes, and `arrivalSpent` (pure): the arrival is spent when the opening is replaced by another picture or the first picture comes in plain. `StylePicker`: the tile's own fade (`fx-arrive`) is gone, with its rule. `check_motion_flow.mjs`: two new groups, `slider` (11 checks: a phone upright, sideways, a large phone sideways, a page zoomed to 200 percent, a short laptop window, a small phone; a slider 40 percent in the window, one with a few pixels in it, one outside it; a window that grows; a phone turned sideways before the slider was seen) and `arrival` (4: the arrival true while the opening runs and spent after it, spent by a style chosen while it runs, read from the `arrive` prop on the React fibre, a decode of 1.8 s leaves the hairline and one status in all its frames), the `loops` check asks for no black frame at all (it allowed 15), and the `shift` sampler leaves out what lasts 1 ms or less (see below). `check_budget.mjs`: the tools term follows the shared chunk (+157 B JS, -10 B CSS), so the landing's own numbers are those of section 29 (+7655 B JS, 345 B under the hard stop; +7179 B CSS).

**Counts that moved (none lowered).** `ts` 442 to **452** (+10: the in-view rule, the largest view always enough, a slider mostly out of the window, the half share, the slider's source, the spent arrival, ArtImage's source, the waiting frame, the result's wiring, no `fx-arrive`); `check:motion:flow` 49 to **64** (slider 11, arrival 4). Every other count is as in section 29.

**Proof that the new checks see the defects.** Run against the build of `57a275b` (the reviewed tip), `slider` fails 7 checks (the three sideways and zoomed windows twice, by themselves and scrolled into the middle: the handle is at 92 two seconds after the slider came and six seconds after it was scrolled to the middle of the window; and the part visible slider sweeps at once) and `arrival` fails 2 (the arrival stays `true` after a style chosen while the opening ran; 110 of 154 frames of a 1.8 s decode have no hairline and no status). Against the fixed build, with the resize listener cut out of `CompareSlider`, the turned phone fails (92 and 92). A leaked loop under reduced motion (the mutation in the header of the script) still fails `shift` on `rotate @ wk-arc` after the sampler's change.

**The `shift` sampler.** The clamp of reduced motion gives every transition .01 ms, so every style change of an element (the buy card's "Save preview" link when the preview arrives, a line that changes class) starts a transition that ends in the frame it starts in. `document.getAnimations()` flushes style, so a tick of the sampler that falls between a commit and its frame sees it: chance, about one run in three on the parent (`831634f`, caught 2 of 5 runs by hand) and more often on this one. The gate "nothing at all under reduced motion" therefore failed on a tree that changed nothing about reduced motion. The sampler now leaves out animations of 1 ms or less; a real animation (duration above 1 ms) is still caught.

**Bytes** (first-load gzip, `5c9054c` before the motion against this tree): /try JS +2,598 B (it was +2,295: the slider's rule, the spent arrival and the waiting marks), CSS +1,906 B (+1,916 before: the tile fade left); /order JS +1,900 B (+1,740), CSS +1,906 B; both are inside the budgets (+4,096 JS, +2,048 CSS). The landing's build is byte for byte the build of the parent: `index.html`, every landing chunk, the four legal pages and `admin.html` have the same hashes (only `checkout-*`, `try-*`, `order-*`, `try.html` and `order.html` differ), so the Chrome checks of the landing and the legal views are those of the parent by construction; `legal_look compare` against the build before the motion: identical, 36 views.

**Browser and gates.** `check:motion:flow` 64 of 64 (about nine minutes), `tsc` clean, `oxlint` 0 errors in everything this work touched and 43 warnings, as before (three errors of `suites/ts/landing_tiles.test.ts:216`, `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks` on a `probe` function that calls hooks, come from `831634f`, the I2 fix, and are not part of this work), `npm run build` exit 0 in preview mode and with `VERCEL_ENV=production` (registry hash `76ffb8838041`), `check:prices`, `check:texts`, `check:styles`, `check:assets` (release gate, 16 tiles), `check:taste` and `check:budget` ok.

**Full set** on a `git archive` export of `f4baf61` (the private fixtures, three suites at a time, no LOCAL environment, scratch on drive D): **36 of 36 green, every count equal to `suites/baseline.json`** in one uninterrupted run (`ts` 452 is the one that moved; `v3reg` 216, `v3cutover` 74, `v3picker` 30, `v3checkout` 128, `v3reveal` 72, `v3plates` 112, `v3wp0` 93, `v3compose` 114, `admin` and `oldadmin` 186 each). It is the one uninterrupted full run that section 28 left to the next builder, on the tip of the motion.

## 31. The review fixes of the release (2026-10-07): four lenses on `9650066`, the fixes, what moved

Four verification lenses (honesty, production, regression and security, performance and accessibility) read the tip `9650066` of `v3-release`: every one said ship, none found a blocker. Their majors and the cheap minors are fixed in this change; what is not code (the plates upload, the Vercel plan and firewall rule, Stripe, Resend, the counsel and native readers, the prepaid Gemini project) stays in the owner's list in the README runbook. The new checks are `scripts/styles_tests/test_release_fixes.py` (suite `v3rel`, 41) and `suites/ts/release_fixes.test.ts` (inside `ts`).

**What the change does.**
* The key and the model: `iris._key()` reads the key file of the owner's machine only with `SNAPEYES_DEV_KEYFILE=1` (a local run that asks the model needs it or `GEMINI_API_KEY`), `suites/run_all.sh` closes the network and drops the keys before a suite starts, and a refused key (401, 403, the 400 of an invalid key) is `ModelDenied`: asked once, answered 503 `model_denied` in the page's language (a busy model still says busy), and `/api/health` says what this instance saw of the model (denied, ok, unknown) and whether the ticket and admin secrets are long enough.
* The order: a refund now ends the making by the page as it ends the server's chain (409 `refunded`; the order stays in the index of unfinished paid orders until the daily run drops it), the ready card shows a 1,600 px display copy made beside the file (`display_url`, the download stays the 4096 px file), a new eye for an order that already went to Stripe closes its open checkout pages, a missing image is a 400, and the 409 for missing plates names the style and the reason, never a plate id.
* The health: `plates_4k` asks for the first and the last plate of every family the visible styles fetch and keeps a good answer 60 s per instance (a `BoundedCache`: v3core rule c).
* The pages: a failsafe message and a `noscript` for /try and /order (a failed script was a blank page), the before and after slider is a keyboard slider (`role=slider`, arrows, Home, End), focus follows the step and an error is an alert, the tools' grey text meets 4.5:1, forced colours keep the menu button and the gold buttons, tile names wrap, /try has the colour scheme, the theme colour and the icon, `vercel.json` sets nosniff, frame denial and a referrer policy.
* The honesty: the sentence about AI-made material from the shared library is on the landing's Reveal box and in the result of /try, word for word in the register of the page (`AI_MATERIAL_PAGE`, `check:texts` item 12), the two Universe tiles that the engine does not sell are gone (14 tiles), the share card carries no count, the price line carries no price while it is held (`HELD_PRICE`), and the captions of the example pictures say Photo, Iris, Art.

## 32. The conditional second restoration (2026-10-12): the free preview asks the model once more when the lid rule fails

The owner allowed a second restoration for the PREVIEW only (the paid 4K master never runs twice). `/api/enhance` measures the first restoration's profile as before; when the lid rule failed (`RETRY_RULES`) and 32 s of the 52 s budget are left it makes the whole draw once more (the model is not seeded, so every call is a new draw), measures it the same way and keeps the second only when it fails no rule the first passed and its severity (`gate.severity`: each failing rule costs its worst arm, a number over its threshold, at least 1.0; a pass costs 0) is at most 0.8 of the first's. A passing eye makes no second call. `SNAPEYES_RETRY2=off` (read once at import, BOM safe) restores the old code byte for byte. The reply gets `retry` and the event `retry`, `gate1`, `sev1`, `sev2`, `retry_ms` only when a rule failed; `events.add` counts a second image call from `retry_ms`.

**Time margin.** A draw after the model call takes 2.0 s on average and 2.3 s at most here (the profile 1.1 s of it), the Reveal 0.4 s and the seals 0.15 s; at Vercel's slow factor of 1.67 that is 3.8 s and 1.0 s. The slowest of the 118 flash calls of the bake-off took 20.2 s (p90 16.1, p99 19.2). So a second restoration needs 20.2 + 3.8 + 1.0 = 25 s and `RETRY_MIN_LEFT` is 32 s (7 s of safety); the p90 of a whole live enhance is 17 s of the 52 s, so a retry starts unless the first draw took over about 20 s.

**What was proven.**
* Identity (`/api/enhance` on the 59 saved eyes of the bake-off, the model answering from the saved restorations, three trees): the old tree and the new one with the switch off agree on all 59 in the reply's keys, the sealed clean JPEG, the display copy, the two compose copies, the profile, the colour QA, the fidelity, the Reveal and the event; with the switch on, the 42 eyes that pass the lid rule agree with the old tree the same way and make one model call; of the 17 that fail it, 9 keep the first restoration (the same pictures as the old tree, only the `retry` field and the event differ) and 8 take the second.
* The new suite `v3retry2` (70 checks): the severity arithmetic, the decision (a pass, a clearly lower severity, not clearly lower, worse, equal, a rule the first passed is never lost, a failing or unmeasurable second call, the time guard at 31.9 and 32.0 s), `/api/enhance` end to end with the real gate on the synthetic irises (failing first and passing second, both failing, a passing first with the same bytes as the switch off, the switch off, too little time, the reply field, the event line, the trigger rules, the 18 spellings of the switch).
* Paid check, 40 of the 59 eyes (all dark brown and grey, 22 of the own ones), the real model, the round 3 code: the first restoration is the paid first call, the retry arm reuses it, so the pair differs only by the retry. First run passes the lid rule 31 of 40; after the retry 35 of 40; 9 retried, 4 fixed, 0 lost, 27 of 27 eyes that passed both rules came back byte for byte as before. Cost 3.63 USD of the 7 (53 image calls, 26 judge calls). The full table, by colour class, is in the owner's report of this work.

**Full set**, the private fixtures, three suites at a time, scratch on drive D: **38 of 38 green, every count equal to `suites/baseline.json`** in one uninterrupted run (the 37 older entries unchanged, the new `v3retry2` 70); `python suites/compile_matrix.py`: compileall `api` ok under Python 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14, 100 files parse as 3.12.

**Not measured.** The behaviour on Vercel (no function of this tree ran there); the model's latency from fra1; a customer's phone photos (the 59 are macro photos and the owner's); more than one retry per eye (there is none); how the retried previews sell.

## 33. The nonce in the work ticket (2026-10-13): two analyses in one second no longer share an order

`iris.mint_ticket` built `<kind>.<expiry>.<signature>` with the expiry in whole seconds, so two `/api/analyze` calls in the same second returned the same work ticket. `pay.order_for_ticket` keeps one unpaid order per ticket (the marker `ticketuse/<day>/<hash of the ticket string>.json`), so the second customer's first draft was handed the first customer's order and access key: the second draft could overwrite the first customer's eye, and the second customer held the key to the first's order. It takes two analyses in one second, so it was rare, but it is a privacy bug.

**What the change does.** `mint_ticket` makes `<kind>.<expiry>.<nonce>.<signature>`: the nonce is 16 hex characters from `secrets.token_hex(8)` and the same HMAC (32 hex) signs `<kind>.<expiry>.<nonce>`. `check_ticket` takes both forms by the same rules (the kind, the expiry up to its last second, `hmac.compare_digest`): the number of fields decides the form and the signature covers every field before it, so a nonce dropped from a new ticket or added to an old one is refused, and so is anything that is not a string. A nonce is any hex of 1 to 64 characters (the minted one is 16): the signature is what makes a ticket good, so a ticket made by another minter with a different width is still taken when it is signed. The three-field form stays good for the tickets minted before the deploy, which live up to 15 minutes (the self-calls' internal ones 5). Nothing else parses a ticket: `pay.ticket_path` reads the expiry as the second field (the same in both forms) and hashes the whole string, which now differs for every ticket; the pages keep the ticket as an opaque string of letters, digits, `.` and `-`; the admin keys (`ops.py`, `scripts/mint_admin.py`, `src/admin/api.ts`) and the price-test tokens (`abtest.py`, `src/shared/pricing.ts`) are other schemes with their own patterns and keys and are not touched. The two copies of the harness made the old form by hand in `fresh_ticket` (one expiry per ticket, to get unique tickets): they now call `mint_ticket`, so every suite that drafts an order runs with the new form. The two admin suites built the admin-key-shaped ticket of "the old scheme" with `mint_ticket("admin-v1")`, which has four fields now and would be refused by the key's shape before its signature is looked at: they build the three-field form by hand (`legacy_ticket`) and still prove the signature.

**What was proven.**
* The new suite `v3nonce` (49 checks): the form (four fields, the signature over kind, expiry and nonce, safe in JSON and in a link, under 160 characters); uniqueness (500 tickets of one frozen second, other kinds, eight threads, `secrets.token_hex(8)` once per ticket, equal again when the nonce is held fixed); both forms accepted by kind and expiry (to the last second) and compared with `compare_digest`; 32 alterations of a new ticket and 6 of an old one refused (signature, nonce, expiry, kind, shape, a dropped or an added nonce, another secret, junk); the matrix of eight kinds in both forms; the places that touch tickets (`pay.ticket_path`, `maker.kick` and `ticket_ok`, the admin lab, `scripts/mint_unlock.py`, the admin key); and end to end through the payments harness: two `/api/analyze` calls in one second give two tickets and, through `/api/order`, two different unpaid orders with their own keys and their own eyes, a repeat of one ticket gets its own order back, a ticket of the old form still makes an order, a changed, extended, expired or wrong-kind ticket makes none, `/api/deglare` and `/api/enhance` gate on both forms, and four parallel analyses make four orders.
* The same suite on the code before the change: 24 of its 49 checks fail, among them 500 tickets of one second collapsing into one, and the end to end ending with both customers in one order with one access key (the second customer's key opens the first customer's order).
* Mutations: 14 changes to `mint_ticket` and `check_ticket` (the nonce left out of the signature, the kind or the expiry not checked, an expiry off by one, `==` for `compare_digest`, the old form dropped, a fifth field or any nonce taken, a one-byte nonce, one nonce per process, a nonce from the clock, the kind left out of the signature, half of the signature compared) were run against the suite: 13 are caught by at least one check, the 14th (a non-string turned into text, as the old code did) behaves the same and cannot be.

**Risk.** A rollback to the code before this change within 15 minutes of the deploy refuses the tickets the new code minted (the old check takes the nonce for part of the signature): an open photo session asks for a retake, and a running chain's self-call is answered 403 (the owner is told once; the order page and the daily run go on). The three-field form can leave `check_ticket` a day after the deploy.

## 34. Pairs design phase 1 (2026-10-14): the finish of the three pair styles

Round 1 of the pairs (27 masters, 9 pair sets in the three styles, drawn by the engine's own art step from the stored 4K eyes, no model call) left four objective defects that the panel and the owner's earlier looks named: a stair stepped, scrappy contact edge on the Kiss geometry, a razor sharp bright hairline on the dark pairs, powder that reads as gravel, and a contact edge of Collision Infinity that is shallower than the owner's H11. This change is local design work, nothing is sold yet. The owner's decisions are untouched: seam mixing counted as before (reading A), `K_STACK` 62, the contact edge preset `ref` for everything but Collision Infinity's valley, no new plate.

**What the engine does now.** Four production switches (`collision.PAIR_FINISH`), each with the prototype's picture as its default and each reaching the pair designs only (`engine.PAIR_DESIGNS`: Kiss, Collision Infinity, Clean Infinity, and an infinity that fell back to the Kiss geometry; the trio, the families and the chain draw as before):
* `crumble_aa` 2.2. The crumbling contact of the Kiss geometry was cut from a noise field at hard thresholds (`ragged_field` over a 0.06 range of the noise, the erosion `smooth` over 0.005 R), so a steep place of the field is a one pixel step and the bites leave small scraps. `compositor.aa_cover` makes the edge from the field itself: the signed distance in pixels (the field over its gradient) through a ramp of 2.2 px, and the broken-edge mask is softened the same way (4 times that). The number of bites and their depth are the prototype's.
* `rim` both. The prototype drew a bright tinted hairline (alpha 0.70, 0.012 R wide, L* 70) wherever the BACK band is dark (L* under 26). A contact reads as a dark contour as soon as one of its two bands is light (the limb of a light front iris is darkened inside, the valley on a light back iris shows), so the hairline is now drawn only where BOTH bands are dark; there it is a soft rim light (`engine.PAIR_HAIR`: alpha 0.34, 0.024 R wide, 0.004 R inside the limb, L* 56). The plan freezes the choice as before (`frozen.hairline`).
* `dust` fine (`engine.PAIR_DUST`). The same particles (the same count, no random stream moves) drawn smaller (grains 0.90, chunks 0.78, atlas chips 0.70 of their size), as rounded flakes (corner rounding 0.42 against 0.15, vertex jitter 0.40 against 0.62), an edge of 2.2 px against 1, a quiet light side (0.12 against 0.20), no dim pebbles (luminance floor 0.40 against 0.22), the atlas shards out of focus by 0.8 px with their shading pulled 55 percent toward flat, and `gain_grain` 1.55 against 1.35 so that the ring of powder keeps about 96 percent of its light.
* `valley` deep, Collision Infinity only (`engine.VALLEY_DEEP_STYLES`): the new preset `ref_deep` (a_in 0.78 as `ref`, `zc_a` 0.98 against 0.96) darkens the back iris at the limb to 0.02 of itself. The shared `ref` is unchanged.

The seam is not touched (P4 below). `ENGINE_V` is 5: the pictures of every pair style moved.

**Numbers, 27 masters, before (round 1, reproduced pixel for pixel by the switches off) and after** (`SP\wave-pairs3\work\evaluate.py`; the metrics are proxies, not a score):
* Contact edge of the Kiss geometry (17 of the 27 masters draw it: all nine Kiss, and Collision and Clean of the four sets with a wide pupil): neighbouring pixel pairs at the eroded boundary that jump from under 0.1 to over 0.9 (a hard cut) 25.9 percent on average (17 to 32) before, 0.6 percent (0.1 to 1.9) after; the kept front iris falls into 10.2 pieces at the boundary on average, 6.8 after.
* Bright fringe at the contact (luma above both sides of the limb, median over the visible arc): very dark with grey blue, Collision +23 and +62, Clean +28 and +67; after, a dark valley of -89 and -33 (-79 and -32). Grey green with dark brown in both orders (-10 to +8 before) is -22 to -4 after. Very dark with dark brown (both bands dark, the hairline stays, now soft) is within 5 of 0 before and after. On the real calibration pair `very_dark` (both bands dark, a weave) the hairline was +65 and +62 and is now +3 and +16.
* Valley of Collision Infinity (the back iris at the limb over the back iris far from it, the references' number of `wave-dg1/MEASURE.md` section 8): H10 0.026, H11 0.017 to 0.018; blue with brown 0.052 and 0.047 to 0.066 before, 0.025 to 0.028 after; grey green with orange brown 0.052, 0.025; very dark with grey blue (the hairline pair) 0.29 and 0.56, 0.032 and 0.027; orange brown with olive 0.100, 0.059 (a dark far iris).
* Powder (flakes of 30 px and more outside the irises, 18 masters): roundness (4 pi area over perimeter squared, median) 0.906 to 0.967, flakes of 2.9 percent of R and over 35 to 15 per master, share of flakes of 1.9 percent of R and over 13.2 to 9.7 percent, the light of the ring of powder (0.09 to 0.7 R outside the irises) 96.6 percent of before.
* The seam budget of decision A is the same number on all ten woven masters (mixed share at most 2.80 percent, support 2.95, the seam band 3.18 against 4, `K` at most 33.3, the stack lens fires on none).
* Cost: the art step of a pair takes the same time and memory (15.3 against 15.3 s and 538 against 539 MB for Kiss, 15.0 and 15.0 s, 554 and 553 MB for Collision Infinity, one machine, equal load).
* Preview against master: the 1024 px preview against the 4096 px master reduced by four, SSIM 0.990 to 0.985 (the pixel wide ramps are a little softer at the preview's size; the design rounds' T9 floor was 0.963).

**P4, the frosted band of Clean Infinity, stays as it is.** A fibre scale, zero mean shift of the blend threshold (0.016, 0.035 and 0.055 R) keeps the mixed share (2.81 percent of an iris before, 2.82, 2.86 and 2.90 after) but changes no pixel by more than 12, 26 levels of 255 and is not visible at 100 percent; the detail preserving blend (`oklab_dp`) keeps a little more fibre inside the band and looks the same. The owner's H10 and H11 have the same straight, soft run (MEASURE.md: the references' band holds 0.52 to 0.65 of the fine detail energy of the iris, ours 0.69 to 1.17); a band that follows the texture would need another seam search, not a variation of this one. Not shipped.

**Goldens that moved** (`scripts/styles_tests/data`, recorded again by `record_goldens_collision_repo.py --designs infinity kiss` and `--real`, reviewed against the old files): `collision_goldens.json` 25 of its 62 pictures (16 infinity, 9 kiss; every trio, family and chain picture is the old hash, so are six Clean Infinity pictures: blue with green, grey with amber, the stack lens, the 1:1 canvas and the words), the seeds are all equal, and the blue with dark brown pair lost its hairline in 11 pictures (the clean one among them); `collision_goldens_real.json` 12 of 21 (10 infinity, 2 kiss; the trio, the family, the chain and six Clean Infinity pictures are the old hashes). `engine_v.json` records version 5 and the new hashes of those two files. Why each moved: the powder of every pair picture with matter is drawn finer, the Kiss contact is antialiased, Collision Infinity takes the deeper valley, and the pairs with one light band lose the bright hairline; the very dark real pair has the soft rim.

**Checks that changed in `test_goldens_collision.py`**, each with its reason: the legacy replay (step A) passes `PROTOTYPE_FINISH` (the four switches off: the proof that the finish is the only thing that moved); the check that the replay can fail patches `ref_deep` as well as `ref` (Collision Infinity draws `ref_deep`); the step B against step A check admits the one clean pair that lost its hairline and lets an edge mode only be lost, never gained; three checks of the master plan flows expected a hairline on the blue with dark brown pair, which now has none (one band is light); two wordings (T10, what the plan froze). Seven checks are new (section 10 of the test): the finish reaches the pair designs only (a trio, a family and a chain are the same picture with it on and off, byte for byte), a Kiss pair draws the same particles, `aa_cover` is as wide as asked however steep the field, the broken-edge field has no stair step, the valley is Collision Infinity's alone, the hairline rule, and the powder keeps its light and its count.

**Not run, not changed:** the Gemini panel (it is uncalibrated and was not asked for), the checkout, the singles (every single-eye picture is byte for byte what it was). The LOCAL check `port_collision.py --check` already reported `engine.py` and `scenes.py` as different on `main` and was not extended.

## 35. The added lid detectors (DG6 step 2a, 2026-10-13): prepared behind SNAPEYES_GATE2, off by default

The conditional second restoration (section 32) retries only when the lid rule FAILS, so a restoration that wrongly PASSES keeps its lashes or its skin: the A12 run found the grey eye cm013 passing the gate with lashes and skin in the disc in every style. The owner has NOT approved any change of what passes, so this work prepares a better detector in the lid gate and ships it OFF. `api/_lib/styles/gate.py` gets three more ways for the lid rule to fail, measured on the same canonical 256 grade (0.03 s, numpy only), each against the eye's OWN median colour or window so that deep furrows and a lighter or darker natural rim, which run all round the iris, are no deviation: `lid_colour_wedge` (75 or more of the 360 degrees of the annulus 0.70-0.88 R more than dE00 12 from the annulus's median colour), `lid_lash_lines` (the share of thin dark lines, a 7 px black top-hat of L* deeper than 10, in the worst 15 degree window of 0.84-0.99 R is 0.22 or more above the median window) and `lid_ring_structure` (the mean squared radial component of the L* gradient, an edge that runs round the iris, 21 or more above the median window). The codes are appended to the lid rule's reason list; the sealed form, its four values and every seal keep their shape (`valid_form` accepts the three codes whether the switch is on or not). `SNAPEYES_GATE2` is read once at import and BOM safe, in `api/_lib/iris.py` (`GATE2_ON`, next to `QA_TONE_ON`: the style package reads no environment, `test_gate_seal.py` forbids `os` in its modules, and `gate.lid` asks `L.GATE2_ON`): unset, empty, `off`, `0`, `false`, `no` is off. No threshold of the five old arms moves.

**What was proven.**
* Off equals today: `gate.lid` with the switch off is, key for key and bit for bit, a verbatim copy of the function as it was, on the 12 synthetic fixtures and 15 painted eyes, and `lid_extra` is never called; the lid and fill records of the 12 synthetic profiles equal the records saved from the code before the change.
* The new suite `v3gate2` (48 checks): that identity, the 21 spellings of the switch and a fresh interpreter with the variable set, the detectors on painted synthetic eyes (eight clean fixtures and five colour classes with no code; a lash fan, a pink or cyan wedge and a pale lid margin that the five old arms pass and that now fail with exactly their code; deep furrows, a lighter rim, a darker limbal ring, a recoloured inner zone and concentric furrows on all five classes, 25 eyes, that must pass and do; the same eyes turned by 37 and 211 degrees), the gate with the switch on (the sealed form, a profile round trip, a seal made on that is read off, severity, the event), and the numbers of 161 looked-at real restorations kept as numbers (no picture): the decision and the counts the calibration was made on.
* Measured on the 161 restorations one reviewer looked at (46 clean, 41 natural structure, 27 minor and 47 clear defects; the 120 production restorations of the bake-off, the 33 calibration restorations, the 8 stored 4K restorations of the A12 and pairs runs): the old rule fails 33 of the 74 defects (recall 44.6 percent, precision 80.5 percent) and 8 of the 87 clean and natural eyes; with the detectors 52 of the 74 (42 of the 47 clear ones; recall 70.3 percent, precision 86.7 percent) and the same 8: no clean or natural eye is newly rejected. Of the 41 defects that pass today they catch 19, of the 20 clear ones 15, among them the A12 eye cm013. On the 120 production restorations the lid pass rate falls from 74.2 to 59.2 percent, the second restoration is asked for 40.8 instead of 25.8 percent of the previews (0.010 USD more per preview), the retake rate after the retry rises from 14.2 to 29.2 percent and the defects that ship fall from 33 to 17 per 100 previews (clear ones 17 to 2.5). The thresholds were set on the same restorations (leave-one-photo-out: 15 of 20 clear, 1 of 79 clean or natural newly rejected): they are a first calibration, the owner's own labels (the DG6 step 2a labelling sheet) are what should confirm them.

**Full set**, the private fixtures, three suites at a time, scratch on drive D: one run on the final tree: **37 of 39 green**, every count equal to `suites/baseline.json` (the 38 older entries unchanged, the new `v3gate2` 48) except two suites that each lost one check to the same timing race: `admin` 185 of 186 ("order with a wrong key: 403 bad_link and an error event reason bad_link") and `v3checkout` 131 of 132 (the help event of a refused style). Both read an event that `L.run` and `events.answer` record AFTER the reply has been sent, so a reader that is not scheduled in time finds no event (the checkout check has no settle step); this change touches neither path (`iris.py` gained one constant). Investigated, not rerun until green: the same two suites with `v3steps` beside them (the same three at a time) came back 186 of 186 and 132 of 132 in three runs on the final tree and in one run on origin/main. `python suites/compile_matrix.py`: compileall `api` ok under Python 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14, 100 files parse as 3.12.

**Not measured.** What the detectors do on a customer's phone photos (the 161 are macro photos and the owner's); the rate on eyes nobody has looked at (24 restorations of two other models that the detectors fail and the old rule passes are on the owner's sheet); a skin wedge of the same lightness as a brown iris and a thin sliver at the very rim (17 of the 27 minor defects pass); the admin panel's Lithuanian labels of the three codes (`GATE_CODE_LT` shows the raw code until they are added); whether the owner's customers retake or leave.

## 36. Pairs design phase 2, step 1 (2026-10-14): Clean Infinity takes the deeper valley of the owner's H10

Phase 1 (section 34) gave Collision Infinity the preset `ref_deep` (the back iris at the contact darkened to 0.02 of itself) and left Clean Infinity on the shared `ref` edge on purpose. The owner's own plain reference (H10) has a valley of 0.026 and Clean Infinity measured 0.051 to 0.055, so the measurement of phase 1 (`("infinity", True)` in the list gives 0.054 to 0.027) is now acted on: `engine.VALLEY_DEEP_STYLES` is `(("infinity", False), ("infinity", True))`. Nothing else moves: the shared `ref` preset, the Kiss geometry (it is not an infinity as drawn), the trio, the families, the chain and every single-eye style draw byte for byte as before; `ENGINE_V` is 6.

**Numbers** (the valley of `SP\wave-pairs3\work\metrics.py valley`: Y across the front limb, median over the visible arc, the darkest point over the back iris far from the limb at 0.10 to 0.17 R; the five woven Clean Infinity masters at 4096 px, before = the phase 1 masters, after = the same art step with the new list):

| set | front arc | valley over far, before | after | FWHM before | after |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| blue with orange brown | A | 0.054 | 0.027 | 0.040 | 0.040 |
| orange brown with light blue | A, B | 0.054, 0.055 | 0.034, 0.026 | 0.050, 0.056 | 0.048, 0.054 |
| grey green with orange brown | A | 0.054 | 0.031 | 0.038 | 0.038 |
| very dark with grey blue | A, B | 0.055, 0.051 | 0.030, 0.028 | 0.048, 0.042 | 0.046, 0.044 |

H10 is 0.026 (MEASURE.md section 8), so the plain pair now sits on the owner's number; the width of the dark band does not change (FWHM within 0.002 R) and the fall inside the front iris is the same (the value 0.04 R inside the limb is identical to the digit). The Clean Infinity of the orange brown with olive brown pair (set br3, a dark far iris) has no visible limb arc long enough for the measurement and is not in the table; the picture moved like the others.

**Goldens that moved** (`scripts/styles_tests/data`, recorded again by `record_goldens_collision_repo.py --designs infinity` and `--real`, reviewed against the old files by hash): `collision_goldens.json` 7 of 62 (every Clean Infinity picture: blue with brown, blue with green at 1024 and at 4096, grey with amber at 1024, 1:1 and with the words, and the stack lens of grey with amber), `collision_goldens_real.json` 8 of 21 (the eight Clean Infinity boards: grey and hazel, similar browns, very dark, blue and yellow, blue and brown, the brown pair, blue with copper, similar blues); every other record, its facts, its seed and its frozen choices are the old ones, the Collision Infinity, Kiss, trio, family and chain hashes included. `engine_v.json` records version 6 and the two new file hashes.

**Checks that changed in `test_goldens_collision.py`**: the deeper valley is the two infinities' alone (the preset check names Clean Infinity too and the list is the pair of entries), the "step B really changed the pictures" check no longer expects the Clean Infinity pictures to equal step A's (all seven moved: the valley), and one check is new (Clean Infinity with the deeper valley has a darker valley on the back iris across the visible front limbs, 3.0 against 5.5 levels of 255 on the synthetic blue and green pair, and the Kiss pair does not move with the valley option).

## 37. Pairs design phase 2, step 2 (2026-10-14): the crossfade of the woven lens follows the texture

The owner looked at the phase 1 sheets and named what is still weak against his H10 and H11: in Clean and Collision Infinity the two irises cross over inside a straight, soft, frosted band that reads as a pie slice, while in H11 the join runs along the texture. Phase 1's own measurement said the band was as wide and as straight as the references' (MEASURE.md: tortuosity 1.00, transition 0.11 R), so the difference is in what is inside the band: both irises' fibres were mixed at the same weight (a ghost of two fibre fields), and the colour changed along a ruler line.

**Tried first, no gain: a minimum cost path.** The seam line was traced again through the lens by dynamic programming (columns along the pair, rows across it, a step of up to two rows, a cost of the darkness of both irises so that the colour step hides in a furrow of both, the CIEDE2000 step, a penalty for running across the fibres and a prior around the plan's line), with a narrow feather. On blue with orange brown the path moved by at most 0.04 R (tortuosity 1.03, lateral extent 0.04 R against the plan's 0.10), the colour step along it is within a few percent of the plan's (as DG1 had found), and with the narrow feather the seam read as a sharper, still straight edge: more of a slice, not less. Dropped; no line of it is in the tree.

**What the engine does now.** One production switch, `collision.PAIR_FINISH` `seam_follow` `"texture"` (`"line"` is the straight band), reaching the woven lens only. `seam_plan.texture_plan` keeps the plan (its line, its half width, its budgets, the lens decision `K_STACK` 62 is still made on the plan as before) and adds two unit fields on the lens frame, computed on the canonical REF_SIDE grade of the two irises (so the same at 1024 and at 4096, and a pure function of the irises and the geometry):
* `tex_c`, the crossfade of the COLOURS: the plan's weight is displaced by up to 0.065 R along a coarse key (high pass of L* at 0.02 R), so tongues of one iris reach into the other along the fibres;
* `tex_f`, the switch of the FIBRES: blend `oklab_tx` (the detail layers of the two irises, what is left of a pixel after a Gaussian of 0.010 R, cross over the weight of `tex_f`, the colours that remain cross over the weight of `tex_c`), a fine key (0.012 R), nested in the band of the colours (displaced by 0.6 of its half width, its own ramp the rest), so the support of the fibre switch lies inside the support of the colours and no pixel outside the band is touched (T1 is unchanged: the band of T1 is the support of the colours).
The key is where the detail of iris A (the high pass of L*) is stronger and brighter than B's relative to the local contrast, smoothed along the local direction of the fibres (the structure tensors of both irises, taps along it of 0.07 and 0.05 R, 0.009 and 0.004 R across), so the tongues are fibres and not blobs. At the limbs both displacements fade out over 0.14 R: the seam meets the two contact arcs where the plan put it (the lens frame of the two circles has its anchors at the lens sides, where one iris's limb arc ends and the other's begins; the lens tips are not on the seam). The dark edge along the limbs fades from the plan's line, not from the texture. The band of the colours is re-fitted: the largest half width up to 0.8 of the plan's that keeps the mixed share and the support under the budgets. A render with `seam_follow` `"line"`, or with no weave plan at all (Kiss geometry, stack lens), draws the old picture.

**Numbers, the ten woven masters (five sets in Clean and in Collision Infinity, 4096 px), straight band against texture** (`SP\wave-seam\work\tools\proxies.py`, `evaluate2.py`; proxies, not a score):
* Budgets of decision A: mixed 2.42 to 2.80 percent before, 1.99 to 2.32 after; support (0 < w < 1) 2.56 to 2.95 before, 2.09 to 2.43 after; seam band of T1 (the check's own number, over 0.95 R) 2.7 to 3.2 percent before, 2.2 to 2.6 after (limit 4). All lower: the budget is kept with room.
* Ghosting, the share of the iris on which both irises' fibres are present at a weight between 0.2 and 0.8: 1.39 to 1.62 percent before, 0.34 to 0.59 after (three to four times less).
* Fibre energy in the band (high pass of L* at 0.012 R in the pixels with 0.1 < w < 0.9, over the same in the single irises): 0.42 to 0.54 before, 0.57 to 0.72 after.
* Straightness. The equivalent seam (the position of a sharp seam with the same area, per column of the lens): lateral extent 0.084 to 0.102 R before, 0.064 to 0.092 after, deviation from its straight fit 0 before, 0.002 to 0.006 R after, slope 9.8 to 10.8 degrees before, 7.5 to 10.3 after: the macro line is as straight as it was (and as the references', which DG1 measured at tortuosity 1.01 to 1.22). The 0.5 contour of the weight itself, over the width of the lens: 0.93 to 0.95 before, 1.24 to 1.85 after on eight of the ten (gb2 0.98): the edge of the crossfade is irregular now, and smoothed at 0.025 R (the scale of the references' recovered seams) 0.93 to 0.94 before, 0.93 to 0.96 after.
* Fibre continuity across the band, the structure tensor coherence of the picture in the band over the irises: 0.76 to 0.96 before, 0.75 to 0.92 after, no gain by this measure (the fibres of both irises near the axis already run along it; the band was as coherent as the iris), and the orientation just above against just below the band (cosine times coherence) 0.22 to 0.41 before, 0.23 to 0.43 after.
* Cost: the art step of Collision Infinity on blue with orange brown at 4096 px takes 1.3 s (10.2 against 8.9 s) and 54 MB (591 against 537 MB) more, a preview at 1024 px 0.12 s (the plan 0.13 s: `texture_plan`, then 0.4 s of compose at 4096); the cost table of `costs.py` is not changed (its rows are measured under the spike's conditions; the budget is 1434 MB).
* Robustness: every ordered pair of the 23 gate-passing calibration eyes at 512 px with the self check, 506 pairs in Clean Infinity (441 woven, 65 that fall back to the Kiss geometry, the stack lens fires on none): no exception, no non-finite pixel, every check passes (T1, T2, T3 with its floors, T6, T19, the seam budgets), mixed at most 2.47 percent, support 2.62, band 2.79; 169 pairs (every third) in Collision Infinity (147 woven): the same, 2.39, 2.54, 2.67 percent.
* The size of the render does not enter: the fields are made on the canonical grade, so the 256 px and the 512 px render carry the same fields (a check of the suite). The master of an order is another image of the same eyes than its preview, so its fibres, and with them the tongues, differ in detail from the preview's; the band, the line and the budgets are the same. Measured: the same two eyes drawn from the sealed 1024 px restorations and from the stored 4K art masters (two different images of one eye) at 1024 px, structural similarity of the seam crop 0.918 to 0.940 with the straight band and 0.917 to 0.939 with the texture on three Clean Infinity sets (blue with orange brown, orange brown with blue, grey green with orange brown), the whole picture 0.969 to 0.975 either way: the texture seam of a preview and of its master agree as well as the band did.

**What it looks like** (the owner's sheets in `SP\wave-seam\final\owner`, 100 percent crops of the 4096 px masters against H10 and H11 at the same scale): the frosted band is gone at 100 percent; the fibres of the two irises are crisp in the band and run on across it, and the colour boundary is an irregular, fibre aligned edge with tongues of 0.02 to 0.07 R instead of a ruler line. At the size of the whole picture (480 px) the change is small: the brown wedge is still a wedge with two arcs and a soft top edge. **Not at the bar:** against H10 and H11 the join is still more regular and more even in tone than the references', the lens is narrower (d/R 1.33 to 1.50 against 1.23 to 1.25), so the wedge is a larger part of the iris, and the colour gap of the high contrast pairs (blue with brown, delta E 33 to 49 against H11's 33) is still seen as a colour wedge at the size of the whole picture.

**Goldens that moved** (`scripts/styles_tests/data`, recorded again by `record_goldens_collision_repo.py --designs infinity` and `--real`, reviewed against the section 36 files by hash and by record): `collision_goldens.json` 18 of 62, every infinity picture that draws the weave (blue with brown at 512, 1024, 4096 and with the words, blue with green at six canvases, the brief edge, the grey with amber, the dark brown pair, in both builds), and in each only the picture and the visible share of the irises (`vis`, the occupant of a pixel inside the band changes by a few tenths of a point) moved; the stack lens pictures, the seam `hard` picture, the wide pupil pair (the Kiss geometry) and every Kiss, trio, family and chain picture are the section 36 hashes. `collision_goldens_real.json` 16 of 21 (the eight board pairs in both builds); the two Kiss, the trio, the family and the chain are unchanged. `engine_v.json` records version 7 and the new hashes.

**Checks of `test_goldens_collision.py`:** none adapted (PAIR_FINISH and PROTOTYPE_FINISH of `collision_cases.py` have the fifth option, so the legacy replay of step A still draws the straight band, which proves that off is the old picture, and the finish-reaches-the-pairs check covers the new switch); six new (section 11): the switch reaches the woven lens only (a trio, a family, a chain, a Kiss pair, a wide pupil pair and the stack lens are the same picture on and off), on a woven pair it changes the picture and only the seam plan (same design, front, seed, lens, distance; off has no texture in the plan), the texture changes nothing outside the seam (every differing pixel lies in the lens and within the reach of the band), the budgets and the self check hold, the fibre switch lies inside the band of the colours and is sharper than the cross-fade, and the plan is a function of the irises alone (twice the same, the size does not enter, the plan it was made from is not changed). v3coll 123 to 129.

**Suites** (the private fixtures, three at a time, scratch on drive D): step 1 alone (the commit of section 36): v3coll 123, v3uni 142, v3compose 116, v3single 116, v3steps 152, v3checkout 132, v3core 86, every count at the baseline, and `npm run build` exit 0. The full set on the final tree: 39 of 40 green in one run, every count equal to `suites/baseline.json` (v3coll 129, the rest as recorded); the fortieth, `fixer`, met a 60 s connect timeout to its own local test server at its first checkout call (three suites and two renders on one machine) and passes alone, 43 of 43. `python suites/compile_matrix.py`: compileall api ok under Python 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14, 100 files parse as 3.12.

**Not measured, not done:** the Gemini panel (uncalibrated, not asked for), a blind panel against H10 and H11 (the looks above are one reviewer's), the lens width (the geometry of `solve_infinity_d` is the owner's and was not touched), a tone harmonisation across the seam for the high contrast pairs.

## 38. Clean Iris and Celestial Gold are hard (2026-10-14): a failing single eye is held back from the two plain-iris styles

The owner rejected a delivered file that kept lashes and lid skin at the rim (the grey eye B of the L1 sheets). `SNAPEYES_GATE2` is on in production since `c84b24d`, so the lid rule fails more eyes, but an advisory style only warns ("An eyelid or lash is still in the ring of your iris. Retake it for a cleaner result."), so the failing set could still be bought. His decision ("taip, padaryk privalomus"): `solo.clean` and `solo.gold` change from `advisory` to `hard` in `api/_lib/styles_registry.py` (two literals). Powder Burst, Splash and Radiance stay advisory, Universe stays hard with the fill rule, the pairs and the families stay hard; `v3reg` now holds the whole table of 28 policies, so that a later change of one of them is a visible edit of a test.

**The path of a policy** (read before it was touched). The literal is read by `catalogue._problem`, the one place a hard rule acts: a hard style is held back for a set when an eye's SEALED gate value for the style's rule is not `ok` (why `gate`) or is missing (why `reseal`: a seal with no profile, an unmeasured eye). Three callers share it. The tile list (`catalogue.tile_row`, `tiles_for`, `pick_for`) marks the tile `available: false` with the `why`; it is never drawn and never recommended. `POST /api/compose` for one style answers 422 `style_unavailable` with the `why`; in a batch the tile is simply not drawn. `POST /api/checkout` (`checkout.freeze_plan`, the first judgement after the draft is read, before an open session is closed or a new one made) answers 409 `style_unavailable {style, eyes, why}` from the sealed profile of the draft, and `events.answer` counts it as a `help` event with route `blocked`: this is the server side refusal, the page cannot go round it. An advisory style is bought and recorded as "ordered after failure" (`checkout` event, `gate` = the failing reason code); a hard style never reaches that event, so the "after failure" count of Clean Iris and Celestial Gold falls to zero by construction and their refusals are in the demand table. The master step does not judge the gate again (it reads the frozen plan): a Stripe session made before the change stays payable for its 24 hours, and a paid order is delivered as it was priced. The sample eye of `/try` passes the lid rule (the gate with the added detectors off and on), so the demo is unchanged. The owner's laboratory (`styles_lab` with eyes) still draws both styles for a failing eye, with the gate readout.

**What the visitor met, and the sentence that was false.** In production only Clean Iris and Celestial Gold can be bought (the 4K plates of Powder Burst and Splash are not in storage, so those open soon). A single eye that fails the lid rule therefore has NO style it can buy: both live tiles say "Retake eye 1 first", the Soon tiles are drawn, nothing is recommended, and the retake card names the eye, says what is in the ring, gives the tip and the e-mail. But the buy card said "This style opens soon. Choose another to order now.", while every other style that can be ordered was greyed out. Now: `buyState` has a kind `retake` (`onlyHeldLive`: no live tile is available and some live tile is held), `BuyCard` draws `buy-retake`, and `picker.retakeBuy` says, in English, German, Lithuanian and Hungarian, "Nothing can be ordered with this photo yet. To order, retake your eye (see above).": no price, no waiver, no button. With Powder Burst and Splash live the same eye warns on them and buys, and the card does not say retake. The reseal sentence ("This preview is from an older version of the page") named a cause that is not the cause of an unmeasured eye, which Clean Iris and Celestial Gold now hold back too: it says "We could not check this preview. Please retake the eye to go on." in the four languages. The flow stand-in (`scripts/lib/flowstub.mjs`) has an eye that fails the lid rule (`api.setEye('lid')`, the registry's own policies) and `scripts/check_motion_flow.mjs --only retake` looks at the real page in Chrome against the build: the held tile, the retake card, the buy card, no price or button or "choose another", the retake button opens the capture screen (4 checks ok).

**Existing checks that moved** (each with its reason in the file, none lowered): `v3reg` (the policy of Clean Iris was asserted advisory), `v3cutover` (the pick of the one live style was asked with a profile that carries no sealed gate value, which a hard style holds back as reseal: the profile now carries a passing gate), `v3admin` (`gate_by_style` said advisory for Clean Iris; the one eye line of the funnel stays absent, its comment no longer says a one eye set always has an advisory style to buy), `v3checkout` (the advisory purchase on a failing eye used Celestial Gold: that is now a refusal, and the advisory purchase is Powder Burst with the plate of its plan in storage), `v3single` (the compose checks sealed the eye WITHOUT a profile, and an old page's plain iris asked for Celestial Gold and Clean Iris: the eye is sealed with its profile as `/api/enhance` seals it, the plain iris checks about the page's own path set the policy advisory for their block, a new check holds the refusal as shipped), `v3steps` (its orders for the two styles come from plain drafts: `new_order` sets the policy advisory for the checkout, the master plan never judges the gate), `ts` (`picker_state` and `picker_page` named a dead end guarantee that the registry no longer gives, the page fixture's Clean Iris tile is hard, the `admin_styles` fixture of the gate rows says hard for the two).

**New checks.** `v3reg` +7 (the table of 28 policies; a failing eye holds the two with why gate and holds neither Powder Burst, Splash, Universe nor Radiance; a passing eye holds nothing; an unsealed eye is reseal; the pick for a failing dark brown eye is no held tile; production today: nothing can be bought and the pick is null), `v3cutover` +1, `v3compose` +9 (section 3b: the tile list of a failing and of a passing eye, the pick, one style 422 gate, a batch draws three and holds two, the drawn pictures are the engine's own bytes, the compose events of the refusals, the sample eye), `v3checkout` +7 (Clean Iris and Celestial Gold on a failing eye: 409 gate, nothing created, the demand counted; on a passing eye: 200 at the price of the class; on an unsealed eye: reseal; a dark brown failing eye), `v3single` +2, `ts` +22 (the state machine, the rendered page for a failing single eye with and without live advisory styles, a passing eye, four languages for the buy card line and the reseal sentence).

**Full set**, the private fixtures (`SNAPEYES_FIXTURES`), three suites at a time, scratch on drive D (`SNAPEYES_SUITES_OUT`), `npm run build` first (tsc, check:prices, check:texts, check:styles: exit 0) and `npm run lint` (exit 0, only the warnings that were there): one run on the final tree, 29 min 55 s, **40 of 40 green**, every count equal to `suites/baseline.json` or above it: `ts` 470 to 492, `v3reg` 219 to 226, `v3cutover` 78 to 79, `v3compose` 116 to 125, `v3checkout` 132 to 139, `v3single` 116 to 118; the other 34 equal (`oldpay` passed, no WinError 10053 this time). One edit of `test_checkout_styles.py` came after the run began (a settle step of at most two seconds for the blocked help event of the two new refusal checks, which `events.answer` records AFTER the reply, the race that section 35 names for the older check of the same kind): `v3checkout` alone on the final file, 139 of 139. `node scripts/check_motion_flow.mjs --only retake` in headless Chrome against the build: 4 checks ok. The pictures: `v3single`, `v3coll`, `v3uni`, `v3core` (the goldens) and the IE7 checks of `v3compose` are green with no golden moved, because the policy decides who is drawn and never how.

**Not measured.** The retake rate of real phone photos (the 29.2 percent after the second restoration is the 120 macro restorations of the bake-off), how many failing visitors retake and buy, and what the owner's customers do when the page tells them nothing can be ordered yet. How often an eye comes without a sealed profile (less than 6 seconds left in `/api/enhance`, a failed measurement): it is rare by construction, and it now holds the two styles back as well.

## 39. Scenes made for phones (2026-10-17): five scroll linked moments on a window up to 767 px, `check:motion:phone`

The owner's phone had system animations off, so he saw no motion at all; with animations on a phone got only the once per element reveals (nothing followed the finger: the only scroll linked scenes were the desktop's, pinned from 768 px). He asked for scenes made for phones. Five now follow the finger, all under one condition (`max-width: 767px`, `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`, `html.mo` and not `html.mo-fail`), none on a desktop or under reduced motion, the engine (`src/motion/motion.ts`) untouched: **the Reveal** (a pinned scene of its own between the section's head and the slider: the desktop scene's frame, script and three step buttons, the stage being the frame, its two honesty lines and the steps as one row, the wrapper the stage plus ONE screen of scrolling in `svh`, kept clear of the sticky button bar, the artwork layer asked for only within 300 px; the slider stays under it), **the wall plate** (opens like a mat, inset 7 to 0 percent, and settles, scale 1.03 to exactly 1; on acrylic the light passes with the scroll, in place of the single sweep a phone used to get on a pick, which is gone), **the size drawing** (drawn by the scroll), **how it works** (the line through the numerals of the swipe rail drawn by the scroll) and **the closing picture** (opens with the scroll). A scene is `track()`/`traverse()` of the engine started by `whenPhone()` (`src/motion/phone.ts`), writes custom properties on its own frame element only, marks it `data-scrub` once it has a value and `data-run` while it is under way, so that at the end the element carries none of the scene's styles and is exactly the static one. Every phone rule is inside the one media query and starts with `html.mo:not(.mo-fail)`. Not animated on a phone, on purpose: the first screen (the spec has no hero exit and no parallax on a photograph), the swipe rails, prices and legal text.

**What the page does now against before** (390 px, motion on): before, no element followed the scroll (the only inline value written during a scroll was the slider's own arrival sweep); now five frames write their values from the scroll (one or two custom properties each, the four of the desktop scene's own script for the Reveal), only while near the screen. The hero, the styles, the rooms, the close-ups, pricing, trust and the FAQ are as they were.

**Measured** (headless Chrome 390 x 844, dpr 3, touch scroll gestures, CPU x1, x4, x6, slow 4G for the loads; before is origin/main `912e529`): frame times while a finger scrolls the whole page and each scene (five segments, three CPU speeds): p50 16.7, p95 16.7 to 16.8, p99 16.8 ms in every segment of both builds; the worst single frames are one of 33 ms in the new build (CPU x6, the wall segment) and one of 33 ms and one of 50 ms in the old (CPU x6, the Reveal and the size segments), the noise of single frames in both; no long task in any segment of either build, and the long animation frames (CPU x6 only) are 3 in one segment of the new build against 4 in one of the old, none with blocking time; CLS 0.0000 over a full scroll down and up at 390 and 1280 px; LCP (the hero's lounge picture) unchanged within the noise of the runs (x4: 1372 and 1416 ms before, 1352 and 1348 after; x6: 1272 and 1264 before, 1260 and 1388 after); load time blocking 499/522 ms (x4) and 1178/1167 (x6) before, 578/513 and 1203/1356 after. Bytes: JavaScript +635 B and CSS +543 B gzip (the allowance `PHONE` of `scripts/check_budget.mjs`: the page was 148 B and 321 B under the hard stops, so the budget WAS the limit and the allowance is the smallest increase: the rest of the page is held to the same budget as before); pictures: unchanged at first load (171.1 kB of images before, 172.7 kB after, slow 4G: the scene's own iris and photo, 94 kB at 1200 px, are fetched in the browser's lazy window in place of the slider's pair, 92 kB, which lies 1,360 px further down now and is fetched when the visitor gets near it), and over a whole walk from the top to the end of the page 67 kB more at dpr 2 (819 to 887 kB, the 900 px files) and 94 kB more at dpr 3 (the 1200 px files): the scene's iris and photo; its artwork layer (51 kB) is a file the page loads anyway further down, now asked for within 300 px of the scene; the page is 1,360 px taller on a phone with motion (the scene), and its slot holds that height (`css/base.css`).

**Checks.** `npm run check:motion:phone` (`scripts/check_motion_phone.mjs`, ten checks in real Chrome, `--only absent,reveal,fit,wall,size,how,final,failsafe,cls,identity`): absent where it must be, the Reveal's pin and table at five progress values and monotonically both ways, the frame pixel for pixel its layer at rest, the fit in four languages at six phone sizes, every other scene against the design at nine progress values both ways with the end state exactly the static one, the failsafe with a dead observer, CLS 0, and the artwork rectangles (the plate, the closing picture, the drawing's thumbnails) pixel for pixel the same as with the motion off, zero tolerance; mutation proof: six deliberate defects (`--mutate-css`, `--mutate-js`), each fails its check. `suites/ts/motion_phone.test.ts`: 70 page code checks. **Existing checks that moved**, each with its reason in the file, none lowered: `check_reveal` static (a phone with motion has its own scene), `check_motion_sections` wall (the band follows the scroll, no single sweep) and sizes (the phone's drawing is scroll driven), `check_landing` labels (the label of whose eye it is on the phone's frame), `check_motion_close` final (a phone's closing picture opens with the scroll), `measure_slots` (the phone column under reduced motion).

**Full set**: the private fixtures (`SNAPEYES_FIXTURES`), three suites at a time, scratch on drive D (`SNAPEYES_SUITES_OUT`), `npm run build` first (tsc, check:prices, check:texts, check:styles: exit 0) and `npm run lint` (exit 0, the same 43 warnings by file and rule as on origin/main): one run on the final tree, 31 min 44 s, **40 of 40 green**, every count equal to `suites/baseline.json` or above it: `ts` 492 to 562; the other 39 equal (`oldpay` passed, no WinError 10053 this time). The browser checks of the page, each against the final build (byte for byte the one the pictures and numbers above come from), all exit 0: `check:landing` (82 ok, 2 notes), `check:motion`, `check:motion:sections`, `check:motion:phone` (ten of ten), `check:motion:close` (all ok: the two checks of the page transition that were known to fail on Chrome 154 passed on this machine's Chrome, 155.0.8059.84, and nothing was changed for them), `check:reveal`, `check:shell`, `check:budget` (+7852 B JavaScript and +7179 B CSS over the page without motion, the figures of origin/main: the scenes' bytes are the `PHONE` allowance), `check:assets`, `check:slots`.

**Not measured.** WebKit: the pin (`position: sticky` in a tall wrapper under iOS Safari's collapsing toolbar), `clamp()` inside `scale` and `stroke-dashoffset`, and the feel of the scrub under a real thumb are unverified here; a real iPhone and a mid range Android are the next test. The renderer here is software (no GPU), so the frame times say the scenes add no main thread work a throttled CPU notices, not how a phone's GPU paints a clipped 358 px frame.

## 40. Pairs design phase 3 (2026-10-20): the tone harmony inside the band of the woven lens and the rounder tongues, two switches

Phase 2 left one thing for the owner's word, and he gave it in chat on 2026-10-20 ("taip, abu"): a tone harmonisation across the seam of the woven pair styles that CHANGES the brightness of an iris inside the crossing band (and nowhere else), with his H10 (Clean Infinity) and H11 (Collision Infinity) as the references, because at the size of the whole picture (480 px) the lens still reads as a colour wedge with two arcs and a soft top edge. The same session was allowed one more thing if time remained and it was clearly better: the thin, hair-like tongue ends of the texture seam. Both are switches of `collision.PAIR_FINISH`. The first commit of the phase puts them in OFF (`band_tone` 0, `tongue` "thin": every picture is the one of phase 2, and the 27 round 1 pair masters, nine sets in Clean Infinity, Collision Infinity and Kiss at 4096 px, are pixel identical to origin/main `a498855`, decoded pixels; the JPEG container differs only by its metadata), the second turns them ON (`band_tone` 0.30, `tongue` "round", `ENGINE_V` 8): the paragraph "Turned on" below says what moved. Everything else in this section describes the switches and what they do, whichever way they stand.

**What the tone harmony does.** `seam_plan.texture_plan` adds a third field to the plan, `tex_tone`: the coarse lightness of the two irises in the lens (OKLab L, a Gaussian of 0.03 R of each, on the canonical grade, B minus A, faded out toward the limbs with the displacements), so it is the same at every size and a function of the two irises and the geometry alone. `compositor._tone_layers` (blend `oklab_tx` only, so a woven lens with the texture) lifts the DARKER of the two irises' pixels by `band_tone` times that gap times sin^2(pi w), with w the colour weight of the crossfade: 0 where the band ends (w 0 or 1), 1 in the middle, so that no pixel outside the band and no pixel at its edge moves. It is a smooth shift of the darker iris's own pixels, made on the iris before the contact edge's multiplier (the dark edge keeps its depth) and before the split into low and high frequencies of `_blend_band` (the detail layers are the layers minus their own low pass, so the fibres keep their contrast and colour: the lightness the pull adds to a pixel does not depend on the pixel's own value, which a check proves with a white noise texture on top). The plan, so the line of the seam, its half width and the budgets of reading A, is the very same plan with the switch on and off (checked). Cost at 4096 px: 0.06 to 0.09 s for the pull and two copies of the lens window (about 25 MB); the plan field is inside the 0.3 s of `texture_plan`.

**Numbers, the five woven sets in both styles at 4096 px** (`SP\wave-tone\final\evidence\metrics_tone_strengths_4096.json`; the strengths 0.15, 0.30 and 1.0, the last one a test of the ceiling and not a setting: 3.3 times the share the owner approved). The colour step across the seam as DG1 measured it for H10 and H11 (`wave-dg1/base/seam_metrics.py`, known seam: CIEDE2000 between the mean colours of two strips 0.08 to 0.14 R above and below the seam, smoothed 0.12 R along it, median): Clean Infinity bb 31.4, bbs 32.1, br3 16.3, gb2 30.9, vd 15.7 (Collision Infinity 31.8, 32.7, 16.7, 30.9, 16.3) against H11 32.8 and H10 21.5: the high contrast sets are AT H11's step, not above it (the 33 to 49 of the phase 2 report are DG1's own eight board pairs: blue with brown 43.5 to 48.5, blue with copper 33.5 to 37.0). With the pull at 0.30 they are 31.4, 32.1, 16.3, 30.9, 15.6 and 31.9, 32.7, 16.7, 30.9, 16.3: the largest change is 0.06 of a unit (0.18 at the ceiling). It cannot be otherwise: the strips lie outside the band, and a pixel outside the band does not move. The step INSIDE the band (the colours at w 0.15 to 0.30 against 0.70 to 0.85) falls on bb, bbs, br3, gb2 and vd by 0.4, 0.1, 2.9, 0.1 and 4.7 percent at 0.30 (9.7 to 9.2 on vd), by 0.2, 0.0, 1.5, 0.1 and 2.3 percent at 0.15 and by 1.1, 0.3, 9.4, 0.1 and 16.0 percent at the ceiling 1.0 (Clean Infinity; Collision Infinity within 0.5 of it). The mixed share, the support and the seam band are the plan's own numbers, unchanged to the last digit (mixed 1.99 to 2.32 percent, support 2.09 to 2.43).

**Why a band limited pull cannot do what it was asked for, and what the step is made of.** The plateau colours of the lens (mean CIELAB of the two sides): bb blue L 42.8 C 14.6 h 226 against brown L 36.2 C 37.8 h 67, CIEDE2000 33.9 of which the lightness is 6.5 units, the chroma 23 and the hue 159 degrees; gb2 grey-green L 46.2 C 13.4 against the same brown, 31.8 (10, 24, 96 degrees); br3 orange brown against olive brown 16.8 (14, 12, 24 degrees); vd very dark against grey-blue 16.3 (22, 1, 33 degrees). The three high contrast sets are a step of hue and chroma between two irises of nearly the same lightness, which a pull of lightness cannot touch, and the pull is at most 0.30 of the gap on the darker iris's own pixels, weighted by its share of the pixel (0.5 in the middle of the band): 15 percent of a gap of 6 to 10 units of L*. Even a pull that moved EVERY pixel of the lens (outside the owner's rule: no pixel outside the band moves) would take the plateau steps, by arithmetic on the plateau colours, from 32.9, 32.9, 16.2, 30.0, 16.8 (bb, bbs, br3, gb2, vd) to 32.7, 32.7, 13.9, 29.3, 12.4 with 30 percent of the darker iris's lightness, to 32.4, 32.4, 11.0, 28.6, 3.2 with all of it, and to 31.2, 31.6, 15.7, 28.9, 16.8 with 30 percent of the chroma of the more chromatic iris: only the dark pair (vd) and the browns (br3) would move, and they are the pairs that already sit under H10's 21.5. The lens is also narrower than the references' (d/R 1.35 to 1.47 against 1.23 to 1.25), and by the pupils, not by a design constant: the rule of `solve_infinity_d` puts the partner's limb 1.015 R plus the pupil's reach from the centre, the pupils of these eyes reach 0.34 to 0.46 R toward the partner (radius 0.26 to 0.48 R, mostly 0.31 to 0.36) while those of H10 and H11 are 0.27 to 0.28 R in radius (measured on the images) and their limbs run about 0.04 R inside the partner's pupil rim (d/R 1.23 to 1.25 against a radius of 0.27), where the rule keeps ours 0.015 R outside it. A laboratory render of bb at d/R 1.30 and 1.25 (`evidence\lens_width_experiment_bb_clean_480_d1.415_1.30_1.25_H10.png`) has the wide lens and the pupil rims of the references, the pupils intact (the compositor's guard), and fails T1 and T6 (the front iris is clipped at the partner's pupil): the owner's rule, not a parameter.

**What it looks like.** At 480 px and at 1024 px the pictures with the pull at 0.15, 0.30 and even 1.0 cannot be told from the picture without it. At 0.30 it changes 20 900 to 37 200 pixels of an 11.2 million pixel master by at least one level (0.19 to 0.33 percent), 2 900 to 21 200 of them by more than 2 levels, the largest change is 5 to 13 levels of 255 and the mean lightness change of the pixels that move by more than 2 levels is 3.2 to 4.5 levels (at 1.0: 26 700 to 42 400 pixels, the largest change 17 to 42 levels, the mean 5.7 to 12.1; `evidence\change_stats_raw.json`); at 100 percent the middle of the seam is a very little lighter on the darker side, no halo, no mud, no outline along the colour boundary, and the transition is as pale in the middle and as dark at its ends as before. The same scale comparison with H10 and H11 does not move either: the lens of ours is narrow, its colour edge sits between two irises of different hue, and the references' lens is wider with two irises of a lighter, more even tone. **Not at the bar**, and not closer to it: the step across the seam of these sets is already H11's, and what separates the two is not inside the band.

**The tongues.** Where the colour crossfade of the texture seam (`tex_c`) follows the texture, a tongue of one iris reaches into the other as a thin sliver: the key is smoothed 0.07 R along the fibres and only 0.009 R across them and tanh(2.5 x key) saturates over long thin stretches, 0.065 R long. DG1's own measure of the seam of H10 and H11 (`seam_metrics.analyse`, known seam, the raw 0.5 contour of the colour weight) says that is the one thing of the seam that is outside the reference band on these masters: tortuosity 1.04 to 1.97 (seven of the ten masters above H10's 1.22: bb 1.64 in Clean and 1.76 in Collision Infinity, bbs 1.97, br3 1.30 and 1.33, vd 1.25 in Collision Infinity; H11 1.03) and roughness at 0.10 R 0.0033 to 0.0321 (four above H10's 0.0195: bb 0.0215 and 0.0236, bbs 0.032; H11 0.0082). `tongue` "round" (`seam_plan.TONGUE_ROUND`: reach 0.05 R, smoothed 0.014 R across, gain 1.8; the line, the fibre switch `tex_f`, the tone field and the half width are the plan's, the band re-fitted to the budgets as before) brings them to tortuosity 1.01 to 1.07 on all ten masters and roughness 0.0015 to 0.0078 (bb 1.04 and 0.0067, bbs 1.07 and 0.0077), with the lateral extent of the raw contour 0.073 to 0.105 R (0.070 to 0.111; H11 0.137, H10 0.102), the mixed share 1.98 to 2.32 percent (1.99 to 2.32), the support 2.08 to 2.47 (2.09 to 2.43; the largest is gb2 in Clean Infinity, 2.38 to 2.47, limit 2.95), the ghosted fibres 0.36 to 0.57 percent of the iris (0.34 to 0.59), the fibre energy in the band 0.55 to 0.71 of the iris's (0.57 to 0.72) and the equivalent seam's lateral extent 0.070 to 0.095 R (0.064 to 0.092), so the macro line is the same. What it costs: the 10 to 90 percent width of the colour transition on the three high contrast sets (the estimator holds from CIEDE2000 25) is 0.104, 0.089 and 0.107 R in Clean Infinity (0.120, 0.102, 0.124): narrower, because the colour edge no longer wanders over the band, and under the 0.105 to 0.17 R of the reference band on bb and bbs. At 100 percent the brown slivers that hung into the blue on bb and bbs are gone; the crossfade is a soft band with the fibres of both irises crossing it, no frosting (the ghosted share is a few tenths of a percent), no ruler.
